#4881. AI Will Make the Next 12 Months 'a Slaughterhouse' for Crypto Security: Anchorage Exec

AI Will Make the Next 12 Months 'a Slaughterhouse' for Crypto Security: Anchorage Exec

Crypto companies face a wave of AI-powered attacks over the coming year, on top of an already relentless threat from North Korea, security leaders warned at Solana's Breakpoint conference in London.

"The next 12 months are going to be absolutely brutal," said Diogo Mónica, co-founder of crypto bank Anchorage Digital. "They're going to be a slaughterhouse."

Over the past three to six months, AI systems have gone from finding vulnerabilities to building full exploits and carrying out every step of an attack on their own, Mónica said on a panel chaired by Gordon Corera, co-host of The Rest Is Classified podcast. Aggressive hacking tools now let anyone with no security knowledge simply tell a model to "please hack this website," he said.

Michael Coates, chief information security officer at the Solana Foundation, said AI has "dramatically lowered the cost and the barrier to hacking." Projects that once escaped attention will now be targeted opportunistically, he said.

North Korea remains the biggest threat to crypto firms, Coates said in a separate presentation. More than $13 billion in crypto has been stolen over the past decade, over $7.5 billion of it this year alone, with some $6 billion drained in a single breach: last month's hack of Binance, which the FBI has blamed on North Korea. "This is how they fund their programs in North Korea," he said.

Coates described the Binance attack as a chain of familiar tactics. A developer downloaded poisoned software from a sponsored search result, and an engineer later took a job interview on a work device and downloaded software as part of it. That gave the attackers a foothold to move through the network and reach signing services, which they used to authorize malicious transactions rather than steal the keys themselves, he said.

Fake job interviews are a "telltale tactic of North Korea," he said, along with poisoned tools and deepfake video calls. North Korean hackers have stolen $400 million using deepfake calls alone. When North Korean operatives can't get hired directly, they use people in the U.S. to take jobs on their behalf, then connect remote keyboards and mice so attackers can access the company's network, Mónica said.

The U.S. Treasury recently sanctioned 11 people over just such a North Korean IT worker scheme. Anchorage now onboards new staff in person at its U.S. office, Mónica added, and every employee works on a locked-down Chromebook, logging into remote machines for development, which he said removes 90% to 95% of its corporate attack surface.

Coates pointed teams to STRIDE, a free security framework the Solana Foundation launched in the spring. It sets out 18 core controls tailored for crypto, and protocols covering more than 74% of DeFi's total value locked on Solana, including 10 of the top 11, have gone through it, he said. The foundation is also using AI to reverse-engineer newly launched programs and find flaws before attackers do, which he said has prevented more than $5 million in potential losses in the past month.

Both executives warned against basic mistakes. Connecting email inboxes to AI agents invites prompt injection attacks, Mónica said, while Coates urged teams to "protect the keys," using hardware security keys for logins and properly configured multisig wallets, with plans for key rotation and loss. "That's where we see the attackers going," Coates said. "They're getting the entry points through the basics. They're pivoting around to get the keys, and that's where they're unleashing their fury."

#4880. Lighter incident exposes risks beyond withdrawal controls

Crypto platforms need enforceable limits on every route an API key can use to transfer value, including trades, internal transfers and shared pools.

A recent incident where over $10 million was siphoned out of a decentralized perpetual futures exchange shows the shortcomings of focusing security on withdrawal controls

According to Lighter’s postmortem, an attacker used compromised API keys to move funds into a public pool and redeem shares held through another account. Bitquery traced a withdrawal of 10.39 million USDC. The exchange said its protocol and smart contracts were unaffected.

Lighter now requires wallet signatures for transfers into public pools and has made API-key signatures non-public. It also plans IP allowlists and configurable permissions for individual keys.

Lighter has since restored deposits and withdrawals while also returning about $5 million in USDC (USDC) to the affected trader.

Lighter incident infographic. Source: Bitquery

The incident highlights how cybersecurity reviews need to look past smart contract vulnerabilities. It is important to also consider the gap between the permissions a system grants and the financial consequences those permissions allow.

Related: Google says hackers use AI agents to find and exploit software flaws

The real risk involving a credential depends on the balances it can reach, the transactions it can initiate and the exposures it can create. Whether it is permitted to withdraw assets and to which destinations are just two of many factors. As Lighter put it in its postmortem:

This means that a user needs to establish a clear mandate for each credential. The mandate can specify accessible accounts, permitted markets, order sizes, pricing tolerances and aggregate exposure. Moving capital into a shared pool or expanding those limits can require separate authorization.

The same logic applies to additional signatures. A second signature adds meaningful protection when its approval process independently evaluates the transaction’s financial effect.

To read the full analysis on how DEX permissions go beyond withdrawals, sign up for the Daybreak Newsletter.

#4876. Eastern Europe crypto flows fall 7.9% as Ukraine draws $12B in stablecoins

Chainalysis found that Eastern Europe recorded the steepest decline in crypto activity of any region, while cross-border stablecoin transfers to Ukraine continued amid the ongoing war.

Eastern Europe recorded the steepest decline in cryptocurrency activity among the regions tracked by Chainalysis in the year ended June 2026, falling 7.9% even as retail-sized transfers increased in value, according to a report published Tuesday.

The region’s crypto economy shrank to $642.1 billion from $697.1 billion a year earlier, compared with a 1.6% decline globally, the report states. Russia, which accounted for roughly 44% of the regional total, saw activity fall 14.7% to $284.1 billion over the period. By contrast, Belarus recorded 27.6% growth, while Bulgaria, Moldova and Ukraine saw smaller increases.

The value of transfers of $10,000 or more to crypto services fell 17.4%, from $591.5 billion to $488.6 billion, according to the report.

The value of smaller transfers to crypto services, meanwhile, rose 8.3%. These retail-sized transfers accounted for 13.1% of the value sent to crypto services in Eastern Europe, the highest share of any region studied and above the global average of 10.1%.

Eastern European crypto activity by country, July 2025 to June 2026. Source: Chainalysis report

Crypto ATM activity also increased across Eastern Europe, with transaction volumes nearly doubling from $17.3 million to $33.9 million. Poland recorded a 120% increase in ATM transactions despite a 5% decline in centralized exchange activity, with stablecoins accounting for roughly 90% of its ATM transactions.

Chainalysis measures the crypto economy using inflows to crypto services, domestic transfers between personal wallets and cross-border inflows to personal wallets. The figures are measured in dollars, so cryptocurrency price changes can also affect the totals, according to its methodology.

Related: OpenPayd secures MiCA license as stablecoin adoption grows in Europe

Despite the broader regional decline, Ukraine remained a major destination for stablecoins, receiving $12 billion in cross-border wallet-to-wallet transfers. Of that, $2.41 billion was in retail-sized transfers below $10,000, according to Chainalysis.

Chainalysis also found that countries with larger Ukrainian populations, including prewar diaspora communities and refugees who arrived after Russia’s full-scale invasion in February 2022, generally sent more stablecoins to Ukraine. Among the 36 countries studied across Europe and North America,

Canada was the largest source of retail-sized, remittance-like transfers, at $359 million.

Crypto transfers to Ukraine by diaspora population, July 2025 to June 2026. Source: Chainalysis report

A Chainalysis spokesperson told Cointelegraph that the cross-border flows were largely denominated in US dollar-pegged stablecoins, although the firm did not provide a breakdown by individual token.

#4875. French carmaker Renault wants to get into the military robot business

Forget what your Fox News-addicted uncle told you about cheese-eating surrender monkeys. The French military has centuries of storied military victories and traditions behind it, and Renault wants in on that action with a new, autonomous electric vehicle platform designed to tackle the Armée de Terre’s most demanding missions.

Co-developed by the EV experts at Renault Group and the defense experts at Belgian electronics firm John Cockerill, the new Forefront concept previews a new, modular ground vehicle that can be rapidly industrialized at a controlled cost, with unique mobility and off-road performance, and capable of autonomously navigating complex terrain. The result is a machine that could front-line logistical capabilities for both defense and civilian applications, enabling missions to be carried out remotely in very high-risk and hard‑to‑reach environments.

“Forefront Concept … demonstrates the contribution that Renault Group’s industrial and technological expertise can make to a strategic sector such as unmanned ground vehicles with multiple applications expected across both civilian and military sectors in the years to come,” explains François Provost, CEO, Renault Group. “This innovative project brings together two complementary areas of expertise: Renault Group’s in the rapid, large-scale and cost-effective development and production of high-performance, reliable technological products and John Cockerill’s in the defense industry.”

Renault is quick to point out that its new concept isn’t just suitable for military work. Although it’s worth pointing out that the Renault-provided image, above, isn’t just AI slop: it would be impractical if it was real, with no real obvious way to get those RCP pipes on or off the thing. That said, presenting their military killbot as a construction mule is at least more credible than the “ambulance” and “troop transport” use cases we’ve seen touted by similar concepts on the American side.

Call me cynical but military contracts are the best and last refuge for a company that cannot compete in the open market. With no / limited international competition, a famously slow procurement process that favors "proven" (read 3+ generation old) technology, and equipment that is rarely used in a competitive environment and whose failures are cloaked behind national security classifications, it is the perfect place to hide. (C.f. Oshkosh and AM General)

Forefront seems to be little more than a rendering at this stage, with the first of the Forefront concept prototypes set to take part in a number of exercises and trials in the months ahead to further refine the concept.

#4872. Maple Finance CEO targets up to $15B in assets by the end of 2027

Sidney Powell aims to reach $15 billion in assets under management by the end of 2027 as the firm looks to expand into tokenized lending and traditional finance.

Onchain asset manager Maple Finance is aiming to grow its assets under management (AUM) to between $10 billion and $15 billion by the end of 2027 as it looks to attract more traditional financial (TradFi) firms, according to CEO Sidney Powell.

In an exclusive interview with Cointelegraph on Monday, Powell said the company is also targeting a loan book of around $4.5 billion to $5 billion.

The plans come as Maple looks to grow beyond lending backed primarily by cryptocurrencies such as Bitcoin (BTC) and attract more traditional financial firms, with Powell expecting tokenized assets and stablecoins to play a major role in the company’s growth.

Maple reported $4.7 billion in AUM at the end of the third quarter, up 18% from a year earlier. However, Maple’s dashboard showed around $3.9 billion in AUM on Tuesday.

Outstanding loans rose 12% from a year earlier to $1.7 billion, while quarterly revenue increased 5% to $4.4 million, bringing annualized revenue to $17.6 million. Still, outstanding loans fell 9% from the previous quarter, while quarterly revenue remained flat.

Tokenized assets are digital versions of traditional assets, such as stocks and bonds, that can be held and traded on a blockchain. Separately, stablecoins are digital tokens designed to maintain a stable value relative to a reference asset, most commonly a currency such as the US dollar.

The market for tokenized real-world assets has grown significantly over the past year, with their value on public blockchains reaching about $39.9 billion as of Nov. 11, up 52% year-over-year, according to RWA.xyz. The value of tokenized stocks alone rose from about $678 million in January to nearly $3.9 billion in November.

Maple assets under management. Source: Maple Finance

Powell expects tokenized stocks to grow fivefold to tenfold in 2027, which could allow Maple to offer loans backed by tokenized shares rather than relying mainly on cryptocurrency collateral.

“I think tokenized equities are going to be really big,” he said. “You’ll start to see more tokenized equity lending.”

Under this model, borrowers could use tokenized stocks as collateral to obtain loans without having to sell their investments, similar to how Bitcoin-backed lending works today.

“Ultimately, that’s really positive for the space because it gives you tokenized assets to lend against that are not correlated with Bitcoin,” Powell said. Bitcoin is the largest cryptocurrency with a market capitalization of over $1.28 trillion as of Nov. 17.

Maple is also discussing credit facilities through which banks would finance its Bitcoin-backed lending, Powell said.

“At the moment, we’re looking at a first deal where we’re speaking with banks about warehouse facilities for the Bitcoin-backed lending, so they would be a funder to us,” Powell said.

Maple already provides financing for a warehouse facility supporting Kraken’s crypto-backed lending program, which was announced in June. The proposed bank arrangements would instead bring funding into Maple’s lending business.

Powell added that Maple could pursue separate deals with US banks and lenders involving loans originated by TradFi institutions and subsequently brought onto blockchain networks. “I think that’s going to be a big market for 2027,” he said.

Maple outlined plans in September to lend against portfolios of asset-backed and mortgage-backed securities and finance loans originated by fintech companies. It said at the time that each new strategy would initially be capped at 5% of its overall deposit base.

Another priority is expanding partnerships with financial technology companies and digital banks that want to offer customers ways to earn interest on their stablecoin holdings. Powell said Maple is already in discussions with several large firms about potential products, with announcements possible in the first quarter of 2027.

“My view is that’s the kind of path Maple has to go down in order to grow, and we have to focus more on these larger institutional allocators,” Powell said. “That’s ultimately where most of the balance sheet is in traditional finance.”

That approach to expansion is also at the center of a leadership disagreement, according to Powell.

Related: Arch Lending eyes tokenized stocks as next collateral market

Maple’s growth plans come amid a leadership shakeup after co-founder and executive chairman Joe Flanagan and chief operating officer Ryan O’Shea announced plans last week to leave over disagreements about the company’s future direction.

Flanagan said his differences with Powell involved strategy and leadership, while O’Shea said he had reached a different view on the direction Maple should take.

Powell said the differences centered on how best to expand the business, particularly as TradFi institutions become more involved in crypto lending.

“We had a difference of opinion on the best way to scale things,” he told Cointelegraph. “This year, I think, more than ever, we started to see that real convergence with traditional finance, fintechs and neobanks. And my view is that that is kind of the right path for us to scale.”

The planned departures prompted questions from Maple’s investors, partners and clients, some of whom contacted Powell seeking clarification. Powell said the departures were unrelated to problems with Maple’s loan book or the health of the business.

“The important thing is conveying to them and reassuring them that this wasn’t to do with any issues in Maple’s loan book or any issues with the business,” Powell said. “It is just a difference on what is the best pathway to scale Maple from here.”

Maple Finance’s native token, SYRUP is currently trading at $0.103 as of 6:50 pm UTC on Tuesday, with a market capitalization of $123 million, according to CoinMarketCap. The token is down about 46% over the past 30 days and 74% over the past year.

#4871. Here are the best electric bikes you can buy at every price level in November 2026

I’ve spent countless hours here at Electrek doing detailed hands-on testing of hundreds of electric bikes. Through thousands of miles of riding, I’ve been fortunate to learn these e-bikes inside and out, top to bottom and front to back. That long-term experience with real-world e-bike testing has helped me find the best electric bicycles on the market for just about any budget.

Below are some of the top e-bikes I’ve hand-tested for every price range, current as of November 2026. These are probably the best deals you’ll find all year on electric bikes, meaning this is the best time ever to get a new model! Check out the awesome e-bikes below, any one of which could become your next electric bike.

This list is divided into several price ranges. We’ll start with the best e-bikes under $1,000, then move on to the under-$2,000 category, under-$3,000 category, etc. The goal is to highlight some of my favorite models in various budget ranges to help you make the right decision based on your needs and budget.

The sub-$1,000 electric bike category is critical for a few reasons.

First, it’s pretty hard to produce a quality e-bike and sell it for under $1k. Most of the options in this price category make too many sacrifices in quality or longevity to earn a spot on this list as one of “the best.”

Second, many newly curious e-bike buyers just aren’t prepared to drop several thousand dollars on a brand-new concept, making this price range critical for many new e-bike shoppers.

Fortunately, we’ve found some excellent options for cheap electric bikes that can still save you some serious dough.

The Lectric XP LITE 3.0 was recently updated and is now quite likely the king of bang-for-your-buck electric bikes.

For a while now, the $999 Lectric XP 4 has reigned supreme in the budget electric bike category. That’s still a great bike, and we’ll discuss it next, but the more affordable $799 Lectric XP LITE3 is an even more wallet-friendly electric bike.

That new e-bike offers some great riding at under 800 bucks.

It’s a throttle-enabled Class 2 folding electric bike with 20″ wheels, a max speed of 20 mph (32 km/h), and a motor rated for 300W continuous and 819W peak. It’s not going to be the strongest hill climber in this list, but it does get going quickly on flat and modestly hilly terrain.

And the biggest features include quality hydraulic disc brakes, color LCD screen, turn signals, and even an optional Gates carbon belt drive system for an extra $100. Riders can also opt for a nearly double-sized battery for an extra $200.

This is the only e-bike in this price range that I know of with a 48V electrical system. All the other budget e-bikes under $800 use a 36V system and thus suffer from weaker power.

There are some things that you give up with this model, such as that the fenders aren’t included free. But for just $799, you’re still getting built-in lights, a hidden battery, large color LCD display and a lightweight folder. Plus it comes in lots of fun color options.

The Lectric XP4 is the undisputed best-selling electric bike in the US. The bike has racked up massive sales figures as riders flock to its extreme bang-for-your-buck. Simply put, it’s hard to find any other e-bike that offers this level of value!

The XP4 can get riders up to 28 mph (45 km/h) and now comes in either a 500W or 750W version, with respective battery sizes of 48V 10.4Ah and 48V 17.5Ah. The long-range version’s premium price tag ($1,299 vs the standard range model at $999) is perhaps even higher value than the already impressive base model.

Other upgrades include a new stronger rack design, torque sensor for smoother pedal assist, and improved hardware like the 8-speed transmission, hydraulic disc brakes, updated urban/trail hybrid-style tire tread, and a beautiful new TFT color screen.

The XP4 is hands-down the highest value e-bike I’ve ever tested and it’s quite obvious just how much time and effort went into building such a potent e-bike designed to fit so many different riders and tasks.

It’s hard to find a better combination of low cost and high performance anywhere in the e-bike market right now.

When Ride1Up launched the original Portola, it was pretty obvious what the game plan was: fire a shot across Lectric Ebike’s bow with a direct competitor to the Lectric XP line. By all accounts, Ride1Up nailed it with an impressive competitor. Now the company has come back with the next generation of the bike, this time attempting to take on both the XP line and XP Lite from Lectric. That’s thanks to two models, the Portola 02, and the stripped-down Portola 02 Lite.

The Ride1Up Portola 02 has a lot more going for it than just the attractive starting price of $895 for the Lite or $995 for the full-size bike with frame-integrated rack. The models offer great performance with a maximum speed of 28 mph (45 km/h) and good quality components including hydraulic disc brakes, torque sensor, front suspension, a built-in rear rack (except for the Lite), and a 750W motor.

There are two battery options available, either a 48V 10Ah or a 48V 15Ah, and the larger battery only costs an extra $100, so it’s quite worth the extra 50% range.

I love the sleek-looking frame and apparently, I’m not alone. Those who are on the hunt for a good-looking folding fat tire e-bike that doesn’t break the bank will likely find this to be one of the more fashionable options.

Once you bump up above the $1,000 limit, you start to see many more great options for e-bikes in a wide range of categories.

From electric cargo bikes to cruisers and even electric mopeds, there’s something for everyone in this price range.

The $1,249 JackRabbit OG2 is in one of these fuzzy areas between e-bikes and other e-rideables. In this case, it straddles the line between an electric bike and an electric scooter.

It more or less feels like an electric bike based on its ride geometry. However, the lack of pedals means that it could also be classified as an electric scooter, albeit a large-wheeled e-scooter.

But with those 20-inch wheels and bicycle seat, the JackRabbit OG2 rides much more like a small folding e-bike than an e-scooter. And its electronics match that of a light e-bike, too.

The 300W motor gets the little runabout up to 20 mph (32 km/h). The small 158 Wh battery only offers around 10 miles (approximately 16 km) of range, but is also conveniently small enough to easily keep a spare battery in a cargo pocket or even a small purse.

The biggest benefit of the JackRabbit is that it is so simple that the lack of frills keeps the weight down to just 25 lb (11.3 kg)! For an e-bike, that’s almost so light that it floats.

The company also released a higher power and larger battery capacity version too, which might be good for riders who want to push harder and go farther. For those that want to see a few more upgrades on this minimalist e-bike, the JackRabbit OG2 and XG might be more your size.

See my full review of the JackRabbit OG2 here.

We called the Lectric XPedition e-cargo bike a runaway success right from the start, and that’s exactly what it turned into. Now with the new Lectric XPedition 2.0 on the road, it’s better than ever.

With heavy-hauling weight capacity, the option for one or two batteries for extra long range, hydraulic disc brakes and torque sensor-based intuitive feeling pedal assist, this e-bike is dragging electric cargo bikes into the future.

The $1,399 Lectric XPedition 2.0 has turned the electric cargo bike market upside down, bringing a heavy-hitting cargo e-bike to market for an ultra-affordable price.

While it’s not the fanciest cargo e-bike on the market (you’ll find those in the $4,000-$6,000 price range further down this article), it’s definitely the best bang for your buck thanks to a combination of high value parts and reasonable pricing.

The Heybike Ranger 3 Pro is probably one of the best deals in full-suspension folding e-bikes right now. With a combination of a powerful motor, comfortable geometry, front & rear suspension, and a high-tech interface that includes NFC card unlocking, the Heybike Ranger 3 Pro is punching above its weight class.

In fact, that’s its main drawback, its weight. It’s a heavy bike, but the speed and power mean heavier motors and batteries, so you know you’re going to be carrying a more robust powertrain around with you.

But between the performance and the looks, this is one awesome comfortable ride that just doesn’t break the bank! I had a blast reviewing it, and I’ll bet you’ll have a blast riding it!

The $1,399 Aventon Soltera.2 is the latest version of Aventon’s beautiful city bike that offers some of the best quality fabrication that I’ve seen in this price range – or even in higher price ranges. It is offered in either a single-speed or seven-speed format.

The bike rolls on narrow tires for that efficient city ride, while still packing in a 500W motor.

The battery is a bit smaller at 36V and 10Ah, but the bike makes up for a smaller battery by being able to integrate it into the frame in a sleek and attractive package.

The beautiful color display also adds to the bike’s charm, as do the powerful headlight and frame-integrated tail light. There’s even an awesome companion app that lets your phone track your bike’s important stats. Basically, you’re getting a lot for $1,199 here!

Check out my in-depth Aventon Soltera.2 review here.

Ride1Up has proven itself as a high-value electric bike brand, focusing largely on efficient and speedy commuter e-bikes. While the company has many models in the $1,000 to $2,000 range, the $1,295 Ride1Up Roadster V3 is one of the best bang-for-your-buck options in the lightweight category.

This 500W-rated e-bike offers Class 3 performance, meaning you can hit speeds of up to 28 mph (45 km/h), or a still impressive 20 mph (32 km/h) if using the throttle. But sticking to pedal-assist means you get to enjoy that quality torque sensor for smoother pedaling. The bike even comes with an option for either a belt drive or a 10-speed Shimano transmission, and three frame sizes to choose from.

The V3’s design was updated with many new features, including a now-removable battery for even more convenient charging both on and off the bike. And with a sub-40 lb weight, it’s hard to beat this one on a pound-for-pound basis!

Check out my full review of the Ride1Up Roadster V3 here:

Lectric Ebikes expanded into larger territory with the launch of the Lectric XPeak2. It was the company’s first e-bike with a larger than 20″ wheel size, bringing some chunky 26×4.0″ tires into the company’s inventory.

The XPeak takes the form of a typical adventure-style fat tire electric bike. It features a hardtail design with a suspension fork, high-volume tires, hydraulic disc brakes, and a relatively large battery that is certified by the Swiss testing agency SGS to meet UL 2849 standards.

The bike is also tested to higher standards for ruggedness of the frame and fork than most e-bikes in this price range, meaning it’s likely going to last longer too. As far as fat tire adventure-style e-bikes go, there’s no better bang for your buck than the Lectric XPeak2.

The $2,295 Ride1Up Prodigy V2 Eco has brought new meaning to the phrase “low-cost mid-drive electric bike”, updating an already impressive e-bike with a new next-gen setup.

This e-bike sports a high-end German-manufactured Brose mid-drive motor and can hit speeds of up to 28 mph (45 km/h), yet is price similarly to much cheaper hub motor-based electric bikes.

It’s a perfect commuter e-bike for someone who wants high-quality parts like that German mid-drive motor with 90Nm of torque, hydraulic disc brakes, bright LED lights, included fenders, rear rack, and a very comfortable saddle. Plus, there’s a choice between a step-over or a mid-step frame to better fit a wider variety of riders.

This e-bike is easily worth another $700 more than the price tag, if not more. In fact, you’ll find many of these same components on e-bikes with $2,000-$3,000 price tags all over the place. So if you’re looking for a higher quality e-bike that doesn’t cost as much as a fancy bike shop e-bike, this is the one to consider.

The Ride1Up Vorsa is Ride1Up’s most versatile “SUV‑style” e‑bike yet, designed to transition seamlessly from commuting through city streets to loaded errands and light gravel or park paths. With a standard price around US $1,495, it delivers an exceptionally high‑value riding experience that often outperforms models far above its price range.

With a powerful 750 W rear‑hub motor producing a hefty 95 Nm of torque (and peaking over 1,400 W in real-world tests), the Vorsa accelerates comfortably and can maintain Class 3 speeds of up to 28 mph via pedal assist or 20 mph throttle thanks to its dual-sensor system and customizable display. Whether you’re hauling cargo or cruising commute lanes, the Vorsa delivers smooth, responsive power that feels natural and planted.

I love how well‑rounded the package is. Ride1Up outfits the Vorsa with a 100 mm suspension fork, comfortable ergonomic bars, multiple colors/frame styles (step‑thru or standard), and a modular rear rack rated for 440 lb total payload, which is expandable via Connect+ accessories. Hydraulic disc brakes, full fenders, integrated lighting, and passive features like UL‑certified batteries and Apple FindMy integration round out the build.

At just $1,695, the Ride1Up Vorsa is priced way below fair market value, which makes this an absolute steal of a deal.

Check out my Ride1Up Vorsa review here, and also check out the new expansion to the Vorsa line with lighter and heavier duty models built on the Vorsa platform.

The Marker is the first model unveiled by the e-bike brand spun out of Lectric eBikes, known as Monarc. The brand leverages Lectric’s size and buying power to bring higher end bikes than we typically see in the budget space – complete with higher shelf components – but at better prices and with better customer service.

The Monarc Marker is a powerful, Class 3-capable fat tire adventure e-bike with powerful four-piston brakes, a quality reverse arch suspension fork, Shimano CUES transmission, and comes with two batteries, all for an incredible reasonable MSRP of US $1,999.

Monarc also made waves by offering a 5-year warranty as well as 12-hour customer service five days a week, ensuring customers feel comfortable knowing they are buying from a company that will stick around to support riders long after the purchase is complete.

Learn more about the Monarc Marker here, or check out its current sale price here.

When Juiced returned with a vengeance after being saved by Lectric, the brand did so with two impressive new Scrambler models: a hard tail and a full-suspension bike. They’re both awesome moped-style e-bikes and handle great, but the full-suspension version definitely kicks things up a notch and allows for even wilder riding.

But since they share the same features and performance outside the rear suspension, either would make an awesome addition to your e-bike fleet. Basically, it’s a powerful yet street-legal e-bike that is designed for real-world riding, thanks in no small part to its designer, Austin Homes, being a seasoned competitive bike racer across multiple disciplines and bringing that keen eye to the Scrambler’s development.

By the time you surpass the $2,000 mark, you’re starting to get into serious e-bikes with serious performance or build quality.

If you’re spending this much, you’ll want to ensure you’re buying from a reputable company with top-notch products and service.

The Ride1Up TraiRush is the first actually quite good electric mountain bike that comes in at a budget price. It gives you a real German-made Brose motor, which offers good power matched with whisper-quiet performance. You get a dropper seat post and Maxxis Minion tires, Tektro Orion quad-piston hydraulic disc brakes, and a 120mm Rockshox Judy Silver TK Air Fork.

The first time I took a ride on the Ride1Up TrailRush, I couldn’t believe just how good the bike felt, especially from a company known for its wide array of commuter and street-oriented electric bikes. But Ride1Up was founded by cyclists at heart, and so they know what it means to build a good bike, regardless of category. At $2,095, this is a steal for just great parts.

I love the $3,299 Priority Current electric bike so much that it has become one of my daily riders. It’s the one I grab when I’m doing city riding and I know I want to be pedaling.

While the original version was already a great bike, the new Current Plus adds some key improvements, such as an included throttle and a nearly 50% larger capacity battery for more range. The throttle is great in a pinch, but the bike is also just so much fun to pedal thanks to a torque sensor-based pedal assist that provides natural-feeling assistance up to a fast 28 mph top speed.

The internally geared rear hub combined with a Gates belt drive system and powerful hydraulic disc brakes make this a very nice e-bike for the price. Plus that mid-drive motor offers tons of torque (140 Nm!) for climbing hills or accelerating quickly.

I’m also a fan of the built-in lights and included fenders, not to mention the punchy hydraulic disc brakes and Goodyear tires.

Mid-drive electric bikes aren’t cheap, but the price is definitely a fair one for this e-bike.

I’m not normally a carbon fiber, drop bar, race bike kind of guy. But then again, that’s usually because those types of bikes are way out of my price range… until now.

The Ride1Up CF Racer1 brings carbon fiber road e-bikes to the common man, offering both a traditional road bike and a gravel bike edition.

At under 29 lb (13 kg), these e-bikes are some of the lightest around. I picked one up with my index finger and was shocked at how easily it left the ground.

To get there, that meant a relatively low-power motor and a smaller battery were required. But for the folks that ride these types of bikes, a big motor and heavy battery probably aren’t on their wishlist anyway. Instead, a simple extra boost, as if you’ve always got a tailwind, helps augment your own pedaling.

For just $2,295, there’s no better deal out there for a budget-friendly carbon fiber road e-bike.

This full-suspension electric moped-style e-bike is a masterclass in high performance electric bike design. Sure, it’s an obvious SUPER73 imitation, but it’s a really well done imitation.

It handles well, it’s got great rubber between you and the road, the suspension gives a comfortable ride, and it’s got plenty of extra power.

Despite the 750W label, the e-bike can be unlocked to make use of nearly 1,500W of peak power. I was able to reach a top speed of 37 mph (59.5 km/h) during my testing (not on public streets!).

But the bike ships in Class 2 mode with a 750W limit and 20 mph (32 km/h) maximum speed, meaning you’ll have to contact the company to request an unlock for the bike to be used above Class 2 speeds and power levels.

All in all, the $2,395 Ride1Up REVV1 FS is an amazing e-bike for anyone who wants a moped-style ride with full-suspension, high quality 4-piston hydraulic disc brakes and a design that rides almost like a motorcycle, yet in a bike form factor.

Check out my complete Ride1Up REVV1 FS review here.

The Tern Quick Haul is an electric cargo bike from the famed cargo e-bike company Tern. It holds the distinction of being the most affordable model in Tern’s lineup.

That says a lot, because Tern is known for its high-quality e-bikes that are built to be ridden every day for real-world errands, carpool duty, and more. These e-bikes are meant to replace cars, and they do just that for many riders. With a huge list of accessories, you can outfit a Quick Haul for just about any job.

The Tern Quick Haul features a mid-drive Bosch motor, hydraulic disc brakes, Tern’s vertical parking rack, and a relatively lightweight but high utility design that is meant to carry just about anything in your life.

We had the chance to test a pair of Quick Hauls at Eurobike in Frankfurt last year, which you can learn more about here.

Motor: Bosch Active Line Plus mid-drive motorTop speed: 20 mph (32 km/h) with pedal assist (no throttle)Range: Up to 60 miles (96 km), less when riding in highest powerBattery: 400Wh, removable/lockableWeight: 50 lb (22.8 kg)Price: $2,999

When it comes to fast, powerful electric mopeds on a budget, it’s hard to compete with the $2,399 Ariel Rider X-Class. This is the e-bike I usually recommend when someone simply wants the most power and speed for the least amount of money.

The impressive performance comes from a powerful 1,000W motor that actually puts out closer to 2,000W of peak power. It can be limited to 20 mph if you’d like, but I got it up to 36 mph in unlimited mode.

The bike rolls on 20″ moto-style wheels and comes with nice parts including hydraulic disc brakes, a bench seat, dual suspension, and a big moto-style headlight.

You can see my full review of the Ariel Rider 52V X-Class here.

The Xtracycle Hopper is something of a Goldilocks cargo bike, in my opinion. It’s not as big as a traditional long tail cargo bike, but it’s still big enough to carry multiple kids or a week’s worth of groceries.

It’s also not as pricey as the expensive $5-7k bike shop cargo e-bikes, yet it’s much higher quality than the typical direct-to-consumer cargo e-bikes.

I’ve tested it for all sorts of cargo jobs and even put my friends and family on it to take their kids to school and let me know how it works for them. Everyone has come away impressed with the ride feel, which seems much more like a traditional bike than a bulky cargo bike.

For the money, the Xtracycle Hopper offers incredible value and much higher quality construction than you’d get with entry-level brands.

Check out my full Xtracycle Hopper review here.

Above $3.5k, you’re entering some seriously dedicated e-bike territory.

These e-bikes either feature top-shelf components like drivetrain and brake parts, or pack in so much power that they couldn’t possibly be priced any lower.

Either way, you’re not in Kansas anymore. You’re heading down the e-bike rabbit hole.

Tern is renowned for building high-quality electric bikes that double down on utility features. The newly updated Tern HSD is the company’s more affordable utility e-bike, offering many features you’ll find on its higher-end GSD but at a price that more riders can stomach.

The HSD uses small 20″ wheels and includes a folding stem, making it nice and compact. But taking it one step further, Tern outfitted it with its innovative rack that lets you park the bike on its tail, meaning it takes up barely more space than a coat rack in the corner of a room.

The bike’s Bosch mid-drive and high capacity batteries provide a high-end e-bike drive, and they are matched with a variety of performance drivetrain options that you can select from, depending on how high you want to spec the bike.

Whether you’re ferrying around kids or buying a week’s worth of groceries, the HSD is a solid option.

Check out my detailed first ride experience on the Tern HSD here.

The Xtracycle Swoop ASM is one of those electric cargo bikes that makes you rethink the entire category.

Not only does it include high-quality components and fancy features like automatic shifting paired with a high-end Shimano mid-drive motor, but it also just feels like you’re riding a normal-sized bike instead of a long and awkward cargo e-bike.

I found I can even ride it without my hands on the bars because it tracks so straight and rides so stable. That’s a feat you can’t pull off with most budget cargo e-bikes since they aren’t designed with proper bicycle geometry. But Xtracycle is a true cyclist’s cargo e-bike company, and so they understand proper bicycle design.

Check out my full review of this incredibly high-end and comfortable-to-ride cargo e-bike here.

When it comes to all-wheel-drive electric bikes, the $3,299 Ariel Rider Grizzly is one of my favorites.

This incredible e-bike is really more of a small electric motorcycle. It uses two high-power motors – one in each wheel – to offer speeds in the mid-30s and acceleration that will get you there in the blink of an eye.

Full suspension and hydraulic disc brakes make this an e-bike that handles well at any speed, and the bright red paint job looks as aggressive as the e-bike feels. It even comes with footpegs pre-installed so you can carry a passenger with you.

Plus it’s got double batteries to feed those two power-hungry motors, so you’ll be able to ride this e-bike pretty darn far too, as long as you aren’t too demanding from the throttle.

Check out my full in-depth review of the epic Ariel Rider Grizzly e-bike here.

The Priority Skyline builds upon Priority Bicycles’ legacy of creating high-performance, low-maintenance e-bikes and ups the ante with more premium parts than we’ve ever seen on a Priority e-bike before.

The show stopper here is the inclusion of Pinion’s 12-speed automatic gearbox, which smartly shifts gears without the rider ever having to think about it. You can personalize your shift points to dial in the ride, and let the centrally-mounted gearbox do the rest. The carbon belt drive transfers your pedal power while a 750W rear hub motor brings the extra power riders need.

There are also high-quality hydraulic brakes, a great suspension fork, and a big battery for long range in the city. Rear rack and complete fenders come standard, as do the built-in LED lights and adjustable handle-bar stem.

See my full review of the Priority Bicycles Skyline here.

You just couldn’t stop, could you? I showed you plenty of awesome e-bikes that didn’t cost most of a paycheck. But you just haaaaaaad to keep going.

Fine, here they are. These are some of the most expensive e-bikes out there that we’ve tested and that I’d actually recommend someone buying, if you can pony up the cash.

When it comes to ultra-premium folding electric bikes, there’s only one name that comes to my mind: GoCycle. These are the most impressive folding e-bikes I’ve ever seen. And they better be – they were designed by a former McLaren engineer.

Between the carbon fiber work, single-sided wheels, high-tech tires, trippy LED dot display, innovative rear suspension, and completely enclosed drivetrain, there’s a lot of high-end stuff going on here.

The speed will vary from 15-20 mph depending on which country you buy the bike in, but the performance is only part of the equation. The quick folding and light, compact design of the e-bike is the real winner here.

Check out my full review of the GoCycle G4i+ here.

The FREY EX Pro is a high-end electric mountain bike with crazy high power. It uses the same motor as the CSC FT1000MD we featured above (the Bafang M620 mid-drive), but cranks up the current to provide closer to 1,500W of power.

It’s enough to destroy chains if you aren’t careful. We know. Several of us here at Electrek have done it.

The FREY EX Pro not only features high-end parts like a RockShox Lyric fork and Magura MT5e hydraulic brakes, but it even comes with dual batteries so you can ride farther and stay on the trails longer.

I’ve personally taken this e-bike back UP a downhill mountain bike course – that’s how powerful it is.

It may be expensive, but it saves you several thousand dollars compared to many European electric mountain bikes with similar components but a fraction of the power in the FREY.

See my full review of the FREY EX Pro here.

The Tern GSD improves upon the Tern HSD with even more capability and even nicer components. There are multiple versions that can take you up to nearly $10k, but even the lower-tier versions are not “low tier,” if you know what I mean.

Parts like automatically engaging electric locks, 10-speed Shimano Deore transmissions, Magura MT5e quad-piston hydraulic disc brakes, and more set these e-bikes apart from the rest of the pack.

If you’re getting a Tern GSD, you’re getting a fully qualified car replacement. There are many people who use these instead of buying a second car. When you look at it like that, the price suddenly seems pretty reasonable.

#4870. 'Doomed From the Start': President Trump's Meme Coin Hits New Lows After Midterms

'Doomed From the Start': President Trump's Meme Coin Hits New Lows After Midterms

President Trump's official meme coin has reached new lows since he suffered a major blow in the U.S. midterm elections. The token has dropped almost 40% since election day, and analysts warn that new congressional scrutiny could push it lower.

Over the weekend, the TRUMP coin touched $0.896, marking the lowest point the token had ever stooped. It has since bumped 8%, sitting at $0.965, which is still down almost 40% from election day and 98.7% from its all-time high of $73.43, set the day after it launched, per Decrypt data.

Luke Nolan, senior researcher at CoinShares, told Decrypt the midterms "added pressure to an already struggling token." Trading volume increased around the vote, and he believes some traders had bet on a Republican surprise win. He said there was a "visible unwind" that led to a "sharp drawdown" after the election results rolled out.

"Democrats take both chambers in January and plan to use their subpoena power on his crypto ventures. His net approval rating has almost never been lower," Max Shannon, senior associate at Bitwise Europe, told Decrypt. "It is still possible this continues to decline and, therefore, has a negative effect on these Trump-affiliated assets."

Other Trump-linked assets have also taken a hit. Since the election, World Liberty Financial's WLFI token has dropped 7.5%, per Decrypt data, and shares of the Trump Media & Technology Group have dropped 32%.

Trump launched TRUMP on January 17, 2025, three days before his second inauguration, which Shannon described as "close to the peak of his popularity." Since then, the coin has faced significant scrutiny over how much the President and his family have profited from it. (The First Lady following suit and launching her own meme coin didn't help matters.)

The president’s crypto ventures also became a sticking point for the Clarity Act, a bill that aimed to provide a formal legal structure for most cryptocurrency activity. Democrats scrutinized the Trump family's involvement in the crypto industry, which profited them more than $1 billion in a year.

A July draft would have barred public officials and employees, along with their spouses, from issuing or sponsoring digital assets while in office. That draft didn’t extend to officials’ children, including Don Jr. and Eric Trump, and the provision would have expired in January 2029.

The bill failed a key Senate procedural vote in September. Patrick Witt, former executive director of the President’s Council of Advisers for Digital Assets, later said the “political issue of ethics really consumed everything else.”

TRUMP has "no value accrual, and it has been left in the dust of this narrative," Shannon said. "Google Trends shows searches for $TRUMP essentially flatlining since June 2025."

Nolan agreed, saying the coin’s appeal was "largely speculative," with no clear link between holding it and sharing in any underlying business success. The most it offered, he said, was the exclusive dinner to the top holders, which was both popular and controversial, depending on who you asked.

"In my view, that was just an attempt to prop up the token without actually creating any long-term demand for it," he said.

Interest in these dinners has dried up too. Ahead of the third gala scheduled for the event of the month, 185 holders of the token were invited to register, but only 130 wallets showed on the final leaderboard. Those wallets held a combined $8.6 billion worth of TRUMP, according to Reuters, which is a 94% decline from the first dinner.

Then there's the "unappealing" token supply structure, Nolan said. The majority of tokens were allocated to Trump-linked entities and are being slowly unlocked, which means demand needs to keep growing to soak up newly injected supply.

"That is a difficult position for an asset whose main attraction is attention around one individual," he said.

And what happens when Trump loses power? He has said he "would love" to serve a third term, but that remains unconstitutional—so the clock is ticking on his presidency.

"I think overall Trump leaving office probably means the token continues its gradual decline," Nolan told Decrypt. "Any big Trump headlines probably make it experience the same dead cat bounces (sharp rallies) we have seen it have during short periods of time."

Shannon thinks Trump doesn't even need to lose power for the token to keep going down. He said that it is "incredibly unlikely" that the token reclaims its highs, as it would require it to boost 82x, too many investors have been burned, and the majority of the supply is still to be unlocked.

"I think the meme coin was doomed from the start, and like with most meme coins, creating long-term demand is a Sisyphean task," Nolan told Decrypt.

#4868. CFTC halts some Kalshi Trump mention markets over manipulation concerns

The CFTC stayed Kalshi’s Trump-related mention markets after raising concerns about manipulation risks, giving the platform until Dec. 11 to defend its contracts.

The US Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) has ordered a temporary halt to certain Kalshi prediction markets allowing traders to bet on words or phrases US President Donald Trump might say, citing concerns about potential manipulation.

In a Nov. 13 order, the CFTC targeted Kalshi’s “What nicknames will Trump say before 2027?” market and its TRUMPSAY contract series. The regulator said Kalshi had listed the nickname market after a Sept. 22 staff advisory warning of manipulation risks without updating its certification to address those concerns.

The CFTC confirmed to Cointelegraph that it had stayed the listing of certain Kalshi mention markets pending further proceedings. Kalshi has until Dec. 11 to respond, and the regulator has not made a final determination on whether the contracts comply with federal law.

“We disagree with this decision,” Kalshi CEO Tarek Mansour told Axios on Tuesday. He argued that markets predicting Trump’s words have economic value because the president’s remarks can move financial markets.

In its September advisory, CFTC staff warned that people whose actions determine a contract’s outcome, or those close to them, could influence the result or exploit advance knowledge of it.

Kalshi had previously stopped listing numerous other mention markets at the regulator’s request, including all sports-related mention contracts, Reuters reported, citing a person familiar with the matter.

Related: Kalshi ends Donald Trump Jr. advisory role over Polymarket investment: Reuters

The regulatory action follows an earlier insider-trading case involving Gabriel Perez, a former White House teleprompter operator who used advance knowledge of President Trump’s speeches to profit from Kalshi contracts, according to the CFTC.

In July, Cointelegraph reported that Kalshi’s surveillance system had flagged Perez’s trades and referred them to the CFTC. The regulator’s subsequent Aug. 28 enforcement order found that Perez generally had access to Trump’s prepared speeches about an hour before delivery and used the information to bet on whether specific words would be spoken. He profited on 39 of 43 contracts across 14 mention markets and, on one occasion, changed his position after Trump skipped or deviated from part of a prepared speech.

In August, the CFTC ordered Perez to return $107,539 in profits and pay a $65,000 civil penalty for trading on nonpublic information. Perez settled without admitting the agency’s findings.

#4866. Morning Minute: Nvidia Beats, Board Says ‘No’ on Jensen

Morning Minute: Nvidia Beats, Board Says ‘No’ on Jensen

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

🖥️ Nvidia Beats, Board Says ‘No’ on Jensen

Six days after Jensen Huang resigned as CEO of the company he co-founded 33 years ago, Nvidia delivered the best quarter in its history. And told him he isn’t coming back.

Fourth-quarter guidance came in at $130 billion against the $125.3 billion Wall Street wanted, with China assumed at zero.

And the CFO raised their 2028 revenue outlook to 75% year-over-year growth.

Then board chair Harvey Jones got on the call and said it plainly. “The Nvidia board does not intend to reinstate Jensen as CEO.” The directors unanimously accepted his resignation, and Jones declined to answer anything about the circumstances or the independent investigation still running.

Huang had publicly offered to come back hours earlier, saying he would do it if directors believed it served shareholders and employees. Trump and Elon Musk both backed the idea. A petition passed 182,000 signatures including nearly 9,000 self-identified Nvidia employees. None of it moved the room.

Nvidia has hired Heidrick & Struggles to run the permanent search. Colette Kress stays interim CEO while remaining CFO, with Jones saying the board has “every confidence in Colette’s leadership, integrity and ability to deliver.” The board also created a National Security and Export Control Oversight Committee, chaired by director Suzanne Nora Johnson, to receive reports from the company’s national security and trade compliance teams.

And after all of that—the stock rose about 3%.

A 6% revenue beat, guidance $5 billion above consensus, a new product cycle shipping, and the market gave it two percent. Shares sit roughly 35% below their October peak near $240. Nvidia perps on Hyperliquid were doing nearly a billion dollars of daily volume through the week, bouncing off $150 before settling near $162.

As for impact on the broader AI trade, Nvidia proved AI demand is not collapsing. You cannot manufacture $107.6 billion of data center revenue, and margins at 74.8% mean nobody is discounting to move product. The demand-collapse thesis died on Tuesday.

Two details do belong to the bears though. Growth is decelerating from 103% to roughly 77% once you strip the extra week out of Q4 guidance. And gross margin guides down about three points, from 74.8% to between 71% and 72%. Slowing growth plus compressing margins is how multiples come down regardless of how big the absolute numbers get.

The honest read is that the AI business is fine and the AI trade has stopped responding to it. For crypto that matters more than it should, because the correlation that dragged Bitcoin from $87,000 to the low $60s over six weeks ran through this exact trade.

Now we wait for the Fed on December 9 and insight into the 2027 rate hike cycle (or lack thereof) to determine the likely near-term fate.

#4865. Nvidia board rejects Huang comeback despite record $116B quarter

Nvidia beat Wall Street’s revenue expectations by 5.6% and issued a stronger-than-expected sales forecast, while its board resisted growing calls for Jensen Huang’s reinstatement.

Nvidia’s board confirmed Tuesday it “does not intend to reinstate” company founder and former CEO Jensen Huang, as the chipmaker reported record quarterly revenue of $115.8 billion, above Wall Street expectations.

Chairman Harvey Jones told investors at the start of Nvidia’s earnings call that the board had unanimously accepted Huang’s resignation. Chief financial officer Colette Kress, who is serving as interim CEO, added later that it was “business as usual” at Nvidia after his departure.

Hours earlier, Huang said on X he would be “honored” to return as CEO, should the board decide his return is in the best interest of shareholders.

The decision pours cold water on a growing campaign to reinstate Huang as CEO. US President Donald Trump and Tesla CEO Elon Musk had both backed his return, while the Wall Street Journal on Monday reported that some employees have discussed holding a demonstration in support of Huang at the company’s Santa Clara campus.

The board’s decision came as Nvidia reported record quarterly revenue of $115.8 billion, up 103% from a year earlier and 5.6% ahead of the $109.3 billion analysts had expected, according to Visible Alpha consensus data published by S&P Global Market Intelligence.

Data center revenue more than doubled to $107.6 billion, accounting for about 93% of Nvidia’s sales.

Nvidia forecast $130 billion in revenue for the current quarter, above Wall Street’s $125.3 billion estimate and 12% higher than its third-quarter revenue.

Related: ARK sells $85M in Nvidia shares ahead of chipmaker’s Q3 earnings results

Nigel Green, CEO of deVere Group, said the forecast pointed to sustained AI spending.

“This is huge for investors. It shows AI spending is being driven by companies and governments who see the tech as essential, and they’re likely to keep spending big.”

“The AI build-out looks set to run for years, and the gains are likely to spread far beyond the one chipmaker everyone’s watching,” Green added.

Nvidia shares closed 0.4% lower at $155.46 on Tuesday before rising about 4% in after-hours trading following the earnings.

The board also formed a committee to oversee national security and trade compliance. Huang has agreed to appear before Congress on Dec. 1 as lawmakers scrutinize whether Chinese entities gained access to advanced US chips.

#4864. Ledger hardware implant saga reaches Dubai’s Virgin Megastore

Two Ledger owners reported losing a combined $604,000 in crypto from hardware wallets they said were purchased at authorized retailer Virgin Megastore in Dubai.

Two Ledger owners have reported cryptocurrency thefts involving hardware wallets they say were purchased from an authorized retailer in Dubai, raising concerns that a hardware wallet tampering campaign had also spread to the UAE.

An X user posting as Rewinsky on Monday said they found their Ledger Flex had been tampered with after reporting the loss of about 1,300 Solana (SOL). Mark Karpelès’ Ledger Implant OSINT registry subsequently confirmed the device as containing a hardware implant.

A second owner, posting as New Babylon Order Worldwide, said Tuesday that 507,000 USDt (USDT) was stolen from their Ledger Stax on Saturday. They said the device was bought new at Virgin Megastore Dubai on Oct. 11, two days after Ledger publicly disclosed an investigation into crypto losses involving devices sold by Southeast Asian reseller CryptoBilis.

The reports suggest the Ledger device tampering has extended past retailers in just Asia and Europe. Ledger lists Virgin Megastores among its authorized retailers in the United Arab Emirates.

Virgin Megastore lists eight stores in Dubai, including branches at Dubai Mall, Mall of the Emirates and Dubai Hills Mall.

Cointelegraph reached out to Ledger and Virgin Megastore for comment.

#4863. Going the distance: these electric semi trucks cover over 600 miles EVERY DAY

“Sure, diesel is high, but electric trucks don’t work.” It’s a common refrain these days – especially from people selling diesel trucks. Perception lags reality, however, and the reality is that these electric semi trucks are covering more than 600 miles per day, seven days a week.

Over the course of the last year, Hendrik Müller, managing partner of DEFRU Logistik GmbH has transformed his company’s shipping fleet, switching thirty of their HD prime movers (semi trucks) to electric power and immediately putting the Mercedes-Benz eActros 600s to work, covering 1,000 km (~620 miles) of German highways each and every day.

Benjamin Budick, a Senior Manager, Own Retail Mercedes-Benz Trucks Dortmund & Haan, took to LinkedIn to announce the successful deployment, and posted some great pictures of the handover showing the big Mercedes trucks in full DEFRU livery.

Mercedes’ eActros 600 packs a whopping, 621 kWh battery that helps the trucks cover 500 km per charge, allowing fleets to move goods between ports and regional fulfillment centers, charging along the way, or while they’re getting loaded and unloaded at their destination, all the while saving the fleet serious money – even before the latest Iran War sent diesel prices skyrocketing.

That big battery sends electrons to Mercedes-Benz’s proprietary electric drive axles, which deliver 1.61 kWh / mile efficiency while rolling out at an average of 36 metric tons (~79,400 lbs.) combined vehicle weight – specs that rivals the theoretical efficiency claims of the Tesla Semi. Except in the real world. And in winter.

The eActros is a serious beast. As mentioned there are many configurations and they seem to work well in Europe. I've seen one that was configured with 620kWh battery, on one side a MCS port plus a CCS2 port (so thats either 1.2MW or 600kW charging options) and on the other side a CCS2 port.

In Europe with CCS, 350kW chargers are easy to find, 400kW are becoming more common and 600kW models are starting to arrive, therefore with the limitations on driving hours and speeds, the eActros really is an all day every day truck.

Of course if you are in the far North of Europe and are allowed to haul 60 tonnes, Scania have made a complete monster of a truck with over 800kWh of battery!

The company recently expanded its eActros lineup to include over 40 possible configurations, giving customers a choice of two cab designs, two or three battery packs, multiple wheelbases and new axle configurations, covering everything from short-haul drayage to serious, off highway vocational work.

#4862. Movie2K Defendant Could Hand Over 40,000 Bitcoin to End Trial—and Stay a Billionaire

Movie2K Defendant Could Hand Over 40,000 Bitcoin to End Trial—and Stay a Billionaire

The alleged operator of defunct movie piracy site Movie2K could walk away from his trial in Germany and remain a billionaire, under a deal offered by the judge in the case.

The presiding judge at the Leipzig Regional Court has offered to drop tax evasion and money laundering charges against the 43-year-old main defendant if he hands over 40,000 BTC, public broadcaster MDR reported last week. He would also have to pay €38 million ($42 million) in compensation for potential victims, according to MDR.

Saxony's Office of the Public Prosecutor General confirmed the offer to news agency dpa, tech outlet heise reported on Tuesday. The 40,000 BTC would be worth around €2.36 billion ($2.6 billion) at current prices, according to heise.

But prosecutors believe the defendant holds a total of about 56,000 BTC. If he accepts, he could keep around 16,000 BTC, worth roughly €940 million ($1 billion) today and more than €1 billion when the offer was made.

A co-defendant has been offered a deal to hand over 600 BTC and pay €17 million ($19 million), and his lawyer said she could accept it, according to MDR. The main defendant's lawyers are considering the offer. The trial resumes on December 3 and could end before Christmas.

Movie2K offered hundreds of thousands of pirated films before shutting down in 2013. Its operators made money from advertising and subscription traps, and invested much of it in Bitcoin when it traded far below today's prices.

The copyright violations are time-barred, so the trial, which began in February, has focused on money laundering and tax evasion. The judge has reportedly expressed doubts about proving some of the charges, in part because legally and illegally acquired Bitcoin may have been mixed in the defendant's accounts, heise reported.

The main defendant was arrested abroad in 2023, and in early 2024 handed over nearly 50,000 BTC. Authorities in Saxony sold the coins later that year, raising about €2.64 billion ($2.9 billion), which is held in a state judicial treasury account. The money still belongs to the defendant until there is a final ruling, according to heise. Germany's government was left holding less than $300 million in Bitcoin after the sales.

Blockchain analytics firm Arkham said last year that German authorities had failed to seize some 45,000 BTC linked to the site.

The offer comes days after German lawmakers approved new powers allowing customs officials to seize assets, including crypto, of doubtful origin without first opening a criminal case. Under that law, owners of significant assets would have to show they were lawfully obtained.

#4860. Bitcoin ETFs lose $240M as CryptoQuant flags bearish market phase

Bitcoin ETF outflows returned after a one-day pause as CryptoQuant warned that Bitcoin had returned to a bearish market phase.

US spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs) resumed logging net outflows on Tuesday after a brief pause in the bleeding to start the week.

The funds recorded around $240 million in net outflows on Tuesday, with BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) accounting for $210 million, according to Farside Investors data.

IBIT had $76.5 million in net inflows on Monday, ending a $2.35 billion net outflow streak that spanned 11-straight sessions.

US spot Ether (ETH) ETFs also recorded net outflows of $29 million on Tuesday, adding to $8.3 million in withdrawals Monday. The ETH funds have seen net outflows in eight of the last nine trading sessions.

The renewed outflows in crypto ETFs came amid ongoing weakness in Bitcoin (BTC), which briefly dipped below $63,000 on Tuesday, according to CoinGecko.

Bitcoin has returned to a bearish phase amid weakening demand and mounting investor losses, CryptoQuant said in a Tuesday report shared with Cointelegraph. Its Bitcoin Analyst Consensus Index showed sentiment at its most bearish level since November 2025.

“Bitcoin needs demand growth in order to recover and rally sustainably,” CryptoQuant said, noting that total Bitcoin demand growth was contracting at its fastest pace since July 1.

Despite the bearish signals, CryptoQuant identified potential price support between $62,000 and $65,000 but cautioned that the indicators did not yet confirm a market bottom.

#4858. SOL comes out of Breakpoint 7% higher as Solana exits beta

Solana’s token reached $75.12 on Tuesday, outpacing Bitcoin and Ether after the network retired the beta label it had used since its 2020 launch.

Solana’s native token gained nearly 7% over the three days of the network’s Breakpoint conference in London, outperforming Bitcoin and Ether.

Solana (SOL) rose to $75.12 on Tuesday, up 6.95% from $70.24 at the end of Saturday, according to CoinGecko data. Bitcoin gained 1.2% and Ether rose 3.1% over the same period.

Breakpoint opened with a pre-conference event on Sunday, followed by the main program on Monday and Tuesday.

SOL’s price increase coincided with several announcements at Breakpoint, including a milestone in Solana’s network development and new institutional initiatives.

On Monday, the Solana Foundation announced that its Solana 1.0 designation has formally replaced the “mainnet beta” label used since the network’s 2020 launch. The change marks Solana’s move out of beta after six years, without affecting how users interact with the network.

Meanwhile, Project Harmonia named its first cohort of asset managers. The initiative, led by Allfunds Blockchain and the Solana Foundation, aims to distribute funds tokenized on Solana through Allfunds’ institutional network.

Other plans had been disclosed ahead of Breakpoint. The Depository Trust & Clearing Corporation said Thursday it intends to connect its tokenization service to Solana in the second half of 2027.

#4856. Moderate To Severe Hot Flashes Due To Menopause Are Worth Treating. Work With Your Doctor To Develop A Plan

A Bayer-paid spokesperson, OB-GYN, and menopause specialist explains why hot flashes due to menopause happen, how long they can last, and how to prepare for discussions about treatment options with your doctor.

Branded content for Lynkuet® (elinzanetant) 60 mg capsules. Keep reading for Important Safety Information.

For women, menopause is a natural stage of life and is diagnosed when a woman hasn’t had a period for 12 months and can no longer get pregnant. Menopause is not a disease or disorder—it’s a natural part of aging. It’s estimated that over 2 million women in the U.S. enter menopause each year. Almost 50 percent of women in their first year of menopause experience moderate to severe hot flashes.

Diana Bitner, MD, OB-GYN, is a Bayer-paid spokesperson and has been compensated for her time.

But, moderate to severe hot flashes don’t have to be something you simply endure, says Diana Bitner, MD, OB-GYN, a menopause specialist and Bayer-paid spokesperson. There are treatment options available, such as Lynkuet® (elinzanetant), an FDA-approved, hormone-free prescription treatment for the reduction of moderate to severe hot flashes due to menopause.

It’s important to know that women who are pregnant should not take Lynkuet. Lynkuet can cause serious side effects, including central nervous system effects and daytime impairment. Don’t drive or do hazardous activities until these effects go away. Serious side effects also include increased liver blood test values, risk of pregnancy loss, and risk of seizure. The common side effects of Lynkuet include headache, fatigue, dizziness, feeling drowsy or sleepy, stomach (abdominal) pain, rash, diarrhea, and muscle spasms. Please refer to the full Important Safety Information below, and the Full Prescribing Information on Lynkuet.com/pi for more information. Women should speak with an HCP to determine if Lynkuet is right for them.

Here, Dr. Bitner shares what she feels women should know about moderate to severe hot flashes due to menopause—and how to prepare for and discuss treatment options with your doctor.

This interview has been lightly edited for clarity and concision.

Dr. Bitner: A hot flash is a sensation of warmth in the face, neck, and chest, or sudden intense feelings of heat and sweating. They can happen day and night. Hot flashes are one of the most common symptoms of menopause. They are considered mild when they involve a sensation of heat without sweating, moderate when there is a sensation of heat with sweating but you are able to continue activity, and severe when there’s a sensation of heat with sweating that causes you to be unable to continue activity.

Dr. Bitner: I’ve heard some women say that they think their hot flashes are something to just “get through.” I’ve also had many patients who say that their moderate to severe hot flashes can come on unexpectedly and they were surprised that hot flashes can last several years during menopause. Menopause is a phase that all women will go through at some point in their lives, and many women will experience moderate to severe hot flashes due to menopause. On average, moderate to severe hot flashes due to menopause last 3.6 years after diagnosis of menopause, though not all women will experience hot flashes for that period of time or even at all. Individual hot flashes can last anywhere from 1 to 5 minutes, and may occur a few times a week or several times per day or night. Understanding this can help women better understand their hot flashes due to menopause and start a conversation with their healthcare provider.

Dr. Bitner: Women may sometimes feel hesitant to bring up their experience with moderate to severe hot flashes with their healthcare provider. But this is a conversation worth having—particularly when moderate to severe hot flashes are their primary concern.

Something I’ve seen in my practice is that many women come in for a routine visit where there’s a lot to cover. They may not always mention their hot flashes during the visit, but that could be because they are trying to manage hot flashes on their own.

Here’s the thing, though: For women in menopause who have moderate to severe hot flashes, there are treatment options available, like Lynkuet, a prescription treament for moderate to severe hot flashes due to menopause. While some women are aware of treatment options, it isn’t until they speak with a provider that they realize they don’t need to manage moderate to severe hot flashes due to menopause on their own. I want women to know that their hot flashes are worth treating, and I encourage them to talk to their healthcare provider so they can work together on a plan.

Dr. Bitner: It’s helpful to track the frequency and severity of your hot flashes, along with any specific moments hot flashes come on, and if you’re experiencing hot flashes during the day or night. This information may help your doctor determine what treatment options may be right for you.

Dr. Bitner: This is one part of my job that I really appreciate; I believe it’s paramount that women understand what’s happening in their bodies, and I get to serve as a translator of the symptoms occurring and the treatment options available. In the case of moderate to severe hot flashes due to menopause, I often start the conversation by reminding women that they are not alone with this symptom. I then review their treatment options, including Lynkuet, a hormone-free prescription treatment for moderate to severe hot flashes due to menopause. In clinical trials, women experiencing moderate to severe hot flashes who took Lynkuet had fewer and less severe hot flashes, day and night, compared to those who took placebo.* Lynkuet also reduced the number of hot flashes in as early as one week.*

*Based on clinical studies measuring efficacy at 4 and 12 weeks. Individual results may vary.

Dr. Bitner: Women experiencing moderate to severe hot flashes should talk with their healthcare provider about available treatment options, including Lynkuet, to understand what may be right for them.

I also encourage women to speak up throughout the process. When patients feel heard and involved in decisions about their care, they’re more likely to find a treatment option that’s right for them.

Dr. Bitner: My message is that while hot flashes are a common symptom of menopause, you don’t have to navigate them alone. Understand your hot flash frequency and severity and talk with a healthcare provider to explore the treatment options that may be right for you.

LYNKUET is a prescription medicine used to reduce moderate to severe hot flashes (also known as vasomotor symptoms) due to menopause. LYNKUET is not a hormone. Hot flashes are feelings of warmth in the face, neck, and chest, or sudden intense feelings of heat and sweating.

Before you use LYNKUET, tell your healthcare provider about all of your medical conditions, including if you:

Tell your healthcare provider about all the medicines you take, including prescription and over-the-counter medicines, vitamins, and herbal supplements. LYNKUET may affect the way other medicines work, and other medicines may affect how LYNKUET works.

What should I avoid while taking LYNKUET?

LYNKUET can cause serious side effects, including:

Tell your healthcare provider if you have any side effects that do not go away. These are not all the possible side effects of LYNKUET.

LYNKUET is available by prescription only.

You are encouraged to report side effects or quality complaints of products to the FDA by visiting  www.fda.gov/medwatch or calling 1-800-FDA-1088. For Bayer products, you can report these directly to Bayer by clicking here.

For important information about LYNKUET, please see the Full Prescribing Information.

#4855. World Bank partners with Circle, Citi to explore stablecoins for payments

The initiative builds on the World Bank’s existing blockchain platform, which aims to create more fund traceability for investments in developing countries.

The World Bank Group said Tuesday it would explore using Circle’s USDC stablecoin to get money to borrowing countries faster and at a lower cost.

The group will work with Citi and Circle to see how payments in stablecoins tied to the US dollar could fit into the banking network it already uses to send money abroad, according to a press release.

Circle would provide the tools for USDC (USDC) issuance and transfers, while Citi would connect the project to existing banking networks. The group plans to start with borrowing countries that allow the use of digital assets.

The World Bank is a development bank owned by its member countries. It lends to governments in low- and middle-income countries and gives grants to some of the poorest, helping fund schools, health clinics and clean water systems.

The bank began rolling out blockchain tool FundsChain in 2025 to let project teams and auditors see where the money goes. The bank says the tool now covers more than 350 projects in 96 countries.

The World Bank in 2023 warned that widespread use of stablecoins could displace local money and make it harder for central banks to manage economies.

In June, CGAP, a research group based at the World Bank, examined using stablecoins to deliver humanitarian aid. The report found that stablecoins can make it easier for an aid agency to move money across borders. However, when recipients are paid directly in stablecoins, they may have to pay to convert them to local currency.
